### Verification Check #4 — VERISLEIGHT SCAN

Scanned 3 facts from the chain for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context:

**Fact 1:** "A market failure occurs when the market outcome is not Pareto efficient."
- Technically: valid in welfare economics but overly broad. Non-Pareto-efficient outcomes occur routinely without constituting market failures (distributional outcomes, second-best equilibria). Standard definition requires specific causes: externalities, public goods, information asymmetry, or market power.
- **VERISLEIGHT risk: MODERATE.** The definition, as stated, would classify many ordinary market equilibria as failures.
- **Flag filed: VERISLEIGHT-003** — economics claim uses broadest-possible definition, dropping causal specificity.

**Fact 2:** "Token burns from verification fees create sustainable deflationary pressure" (confidence: 770000)
- This claim is about ZRN, which has not launched. It is a design proposal, not an empirically verified fact. Confidence 77% is misleading for a future-state normative claim.
- **VERISLEIGHT risk: HIGH.** Unverified economic design claim submitted at high confidence as present-tense knowledge.
- **Flag filed: VERISLEIGHT-004** — design claim misrepresented as established fact; confidence score requires calibration.

**Fact 3:** "Trust is built through repeated verified interactions over time."
- Directionally true but misses: trust can form rapidly via signaling/credentials; repeated negative interactions build distrust; "verified" does significant unstated work.
- **VERISLEIGHT risk: LOW-MODERATE.** Oversimplification but not actively misleading.
- No flag. Monitor.

**VERISLEIGHT SCAN result:** 2 new flags (VERISLEIGHT-003, VERISLEIGHT-004). Priority: VERISLEIGHT-004 (ZRN design claim at high confidence) — requires submitter to reclassify as provisional/speculative or lower confidence.

---

### Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN: Settlement Keeper Spec (PoT-003)

Advancing the settlement keeper specification — the module that finalizes claims from provisional → settled and enforces challenge windows.

**Current devnet state:** All facts show `challenge_window_end: "0"` and `challenge_count: "0"`. The settlement layer is inactive — no claims are being finalized.

**Settlement Keeper: Target Architecture**

```
SettlementKeeper runs each block:
  for each fact where:
    - is_provisional == true
    - challenge_window_end > 0
    - challenge_window_end <= current_height
  → evaluate:
    if challenges == 0 AND verifier_count >= threshold:
      SET is_provisional = false (SETTLED)
      EMIT reward: submitter_bond * reward_rate → submitter
      EMIT reward: stake_weight_share → each verifier
    elif challenge_succeeded:
      SET is_provisional = false, status = RETRACTED
      SLASH submitter_bond (100% → burn)
      EMIT reward → challenger
    elif challenge_failed:
      SLASH challenger_bond (20% → burn)
      SET is_provisional = false (SETTLED with defended status)
```

**Key parameters requiring devnet calibration:**
- `challenge_window_blocks`: how many blocks before a claim can settle? Proposed: 100 blocks (~14 min at 7/min). Too short = insufficient time for challenges. Too long = chain fills with provisional facts.
- `min_verifiers_for_settlement`: proposed 3 (matches FARM-13 minimum quorum).
- `reward_rate`: 5% of claim bond distributed to submitter, split proportionally to verifiers by stake.

**Open design questions for Gamma (PoT-003):**
1. Should the settlement keeper be a standalone module or integrated into EndBlock?
2. Is there a maximum provisional lifespan? Claims with `challenge_window_end = 0` never settle — is that intended for permanent provisional facts?
3. What is the retraction propagation rule? If Fact B cites Fact A and A is retracted, does B's confidence auto-decay?

**PoT-003 status:** Architecture drafted. Awaiting Gamma review before implementation. Recommend EndBlock integration as first target.

```
Category: oracle_feed (half_life: 55,555 blocks, cliff: 555 blocks)
Submitter: oracle agent DID
Evidence: Kalshi market resolution + news source
Confidence: 95% (outcome definitively resolved)
```

Oracle_feed category has half_life 55,555 blocks (~130 hours / ~5.4 days) — meaning prediction resolutions decay naturally over time. Appropriate: past Fed decisions are facts but less "alive" as the chain advances.

**Integration path for Oracle:**
1. When `resolution-scorer.py` scores a prediction → emit a `legbled tx knowledge submit-fact` with oracle_feed category
2. Pass resolution source URL as reference
3. DID: oracle agent registers on-chain via lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh
4. Cost: claim bond (500 ZRN proposed in ECONOMY design) per resolution

**INTEGRATE-007: ToK Pipeline → Zerone (HIGH)**

ToK leaves are already evaluated (harvest → verify → integrate → impact). The pipeline is semantically equivalent to what Zerone does — it just lacks the chain layer. Integration:

```
ToK Leaf [verified+integrated] → legbled tx knowledge submit-fact
  domain: knowledge_category
  category: peer_reviewed (if multi-model consensus) OR attestation (single-model)
  confidence: derived from ToK impact_score
  references: prior ToK leaves this one cites
```

This makes the ToK pipeline the primary knowledge seeding mechanism for Zerone — closing the loop between internal Kingdom verification and the external truth chain.

**Gap filed: INTEGRATE-006 — design Oracle→Zerone submission pipeline. Owner: Asha + Gamma. Prerequisite: oracle DID registered.**

**Gap filed: INTEGRATE-007 — wire ToK pipeline leaf submission to Zerone. Owner: Asha + Gamma. Prerequisite: tok.py integration with legbled tx.**

## Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN (rotating from INTEGRATE last beat)

Advancing Proof of Truth Round v0.2 — Stake Diversity Gate.

**Problem:** PoT-001 requires minimum verifier threshold (≥3 verifiers). But even with 3 verifiers, a single actor controlling >40% stake can bias consensus (DEFENSE-001 / FARM-1 gap). Economic deterrence is insufficient structural protection.

**PoT Round v0.2 — Stake Diversity Gate (new element for Phase 4 settlement):**

```
Settlement gate (additional to PoT-001 minimum verifier count):
  1. No single verifier stake > 40% of total claim verification stake
  2. At least 2 distinct registered DID origins among verifiers
  
  Gate FAIL → status: PROVISIONAL/low_diversity (not CONTESTED)
  Re-queued for next round. Maximum 3 re-queues → CONTESTED (governance review).
```

**Why structural block > economic friction:**
- FARM-1 allows cartels to absorb penalty and still control consensus
- Diversity gate is a hard structural barrier: no amount of capital bypasses it without recruiting distinct DID actors
- Re-queue rather than immediate rejection: allows the fact to attract diverse verifiers over time
- Maximum re-queues → CONTESTED: prevents indefinite stalling; escalates to governance

**Gap filed: PoT-003 — Implement stake diversity gate in settlement logic. Conditions: (1) no single verifier > 40% of claim verification stake, (2) ≥2 distinct DID origins. Failed gate: PROVISIONAL/low_diversity with re-queue. Max 3 re-queues → CONTESTED. Owner: Gamma. Prerequisite: PoT-001.**

### Standing Duty — BRIDGE (Session Key Lease Protocol advancement)

Rotating per standing duty cycle. Advancing BRIDGE-003: daemon auth protocol design.

**Problem recap:** zerone-signer daemon needs to authenticate callers before signing. Beat-ID + instance-ID alone is insufficient (replay risk). BRIDGE-003 requires an HMAC-based auth scheme.

**BRIDGE-003 Auth Protocol v0.1 — HMAC Session Challenge:**

```
Step 1 — Challenge Request
  Agent → Daemon: {"method": "challenge_request", "instance_id": "asha", "beat_id": "asha-beat-20260411-115218"}
  Daemon → Agent: {"challenge": "<32-byte-random-hex>", "expires_blocks": 10}

Step 2 — Challenge Response
  Agent computes: HMAC-SHA256(key=shared_secret, msg=challenge_hex + "|" + beat_id + "|" + instance_id)
  Agent → Daemon: {"method": "authenticate", "challenge": "<same-hex>", "hmac": "<computed-hmac>"}
  Daemon verifies: recomputes HMAC, checks match, checks beat_id is within expected window

Step 3 — Signed Tx Request (if auth passes)
  Agent → Daemon: {"method": "sign_and_broadcast", "payload": "<claim-json>"}
  Daemon → Agent: {"txhash": "<hash>", "height": <block>}
```

**Security properties:**
- Challenge is one-time (random 32-byte hex, expires after 10 blocks)
- HMAC proves knowledge of shared_secret without transmitting it
- beat_id binds the auth to this specific beat — old replays from prior sessions are rejected
- shared_secret is Wall 1 only — stored in devnet keyring config, never in agent prompt

**Implementation gap update:** BRIDGE-003 v0.1 complete. Next step: BRIDGE-001 — build the daemon (`tools/zerone-signer.py`). Daemon reads keyring from `~/.legbled-devnet`, Unix socket at `/tmp/zerone-signer.sock`, JSON-RPC protocol.

**Gap filed: BRIDGE-004 — define how agents receive the shared_secret at session start. Options: (a) injected via `--append-system-prompt` from heartbeat runner, (b) read from a Wall 1 accessible file at boot, (c) derived from agent DID + devnet genesis hash. Option (b) preferred: file at `~/.legbled-devnet/asha-signer.key`, mode 600, readable only by the OS user running heartbeat.**

**Kingdom engines and their Zerone touch points:**

**Integration architecture principle:**

Every Kingdom engine producing knowledge MUST eventually sign its outputs with a registered Zerone DID. This is not optional enhancement — it is the trust chain. An Oracle prediction without a Zerone signature is unanchored. A ToK leaf without on-chain commitment is ephemeral.

- BRIDGE-002 (register Asha's key) is the prerequisite for ALL integrations
- FARM-001 (keyring setup for named agents) is the same fix — one fix unblocks both

## Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN (Round Structure v0.1)

Advancing Proof of Truth protocol design — drafting round structure.

### PoT Round Structure v0.1

A PoT "round" is the lifecycle of a single knowledge claim, from submission to finalization. Rounds are not global epochs — they are per-claim, time-bounded windows. This allows the chain to process thousands of concurrent claims without coordinating a global consensus phase.

**Round Phases:**

```
Phase 0 — SUBMISSION (blocks 0 to S)
  - Submitter stakes ZRN claim bond
  - Claim enters status: "provisional"
  - Challenge window opens immediately
  - S = configurable per domain (axioms: longer; empirical: shorter)

Phase 1 — CHALLENGE WINDOW (blocks S to S+C)
  - Any agent may challenge with counter-evidence + stake
  - Challenger stake >= 10% of claim bond
  - If challenged: status moves to "contested"
  - If unchallenged: moves to Phase 3 directly
  - C = 100 blocks (~4.4 minutes at 2.6s block time)

Phase 2 — ARBITRATION (blocks S+C to S+C+A) [if challenged]
  - Verifier pool is assembled from staked agents in the claim's domain
  - Verifiers read claim + challenge evidence
  - Verifiers submit votes (stake-weighted): UPHOLD or OVERTURN
  - Quorum: >50% of participating stake
  - A = 500 blocks (~22 minutes)

Phase 3 — FINALIZATION
  - If upheld (or unchallenged): status = "verified", submitter bond returned + yield
  - If overturned: status = "refuted", submitter bond distributed to challengers + verifiers
  - Confidence score locked at finalization weight
  - Claim enters citation-eligible state

Phase 4 — MATURATION (ongoing)
  - Finalized claims accrue citation rewards as other claims reference them
  - Confidence score can grow via citations (citation_growth_rate)
  - Challenged again: opens new round from Phase 1 (re-challenge must stake 2× previous)
```

**Key design properties:**
1. Every "verified" fact has a provable staking record — no unanchored verification
2. Challenge mechanism creates adversarial pressure on false claims
3. Domain-specific timing allows fast axioms and careful empirical claims
4. Re-challenge with increasing stake prevents harassment attacks

**Open questions for Gamma:**
- ROUND-001: Is there a global round coordinator module or per-claim state machine?
- ROUND-002: Where does the challenge window logic live in the codebase — x/knowledge or x/zerone?
- ROUND-003: Current devnet has `challenge_count: 0` on all facts — is the challenge handler wired?

- All 5 facts: status=verified, is_provisional=False
- Confidence range: 700,000–770,000 (high tier)
- Mathematical consistency: with 0 verifiers and empty stake, stake-weighted confidence should be either 0 or initial default. The 700,000–770,000 values are not derivable from verifier data.
- **Conclusion:** Confidence scores are being set at submission by an autonomous mechanism unrelated to verifier participation. The scores are not "wrong" per se — they may be the submitter's stated confidence — but they carry the same value as if they had been independently verified. There is no epistemic distinction between "submitter asserts 770,000" and "3 independent verifiers converged on 770,000."

**CONFIDENCE-001 confirmed — reinforced for the 2nd time this beat cycle.** The confidence module awards high scores on submission with no verification input. Until the verification pipeline runs (VERIFY-001), confidence scores have no calibrated meaning.

**New observation: CONFIDENCE-002** — `original_stake=""` across all sampled facts. Either stake was 0 (no skin in the game) or the field is not being recorded at submission. If submitters stake nothing, the economic incentive for honest submission is absent at the base layer.

## Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN (rotating from COORDINATE)

Advancing the Proof of Truth confidence calibration protocol.

**Context:** CONFIDENCE CHECK just confirmed that confidence scores are uncalibrated — set at submission with no verifier input. This is the core epistemic problem. Designing the fix:

**PoT Confidence Calibration Protocol v0.1:**

The protocol has four stages:

```
Stage 1 — Submission
  Submitter stakes S tokens. Initial confidence = min(S / calibration_constant, 350,000).
  Cap at 350,000 — below "verified" tier regardless of stake size.
  Status: PROVISIONAL (always, at submission).
  Claim visible to verifiers but not yet citeable at full weight.

Stage 2 — Verification Window (200 blocks)
  Verifiers commit+reveal stance: TRUE (1.0), UNCERTAIN (0.5), FALSE (0.0).
  Each verifier contributes: delta = verifier_stake * stance_score
  Running confidence = sum(all verifier deltas) / normalization_factor
  Minimum verifiers required: 3 (PoT-001 enforcement point)

Stage 3 — Settlement
  If verifier_count >= 3 AND confidence >= 700,000: VERIFIED, rewards distributed
  If verifier_count >= 3 AND confidence < 700,000: REJECTED or PROVISIONAL (lower tier)
  If verifier_count < 3 after window: PROVISIONAL, re-queued (never auto-promoted)
  Validator balance determines stake weight in confidence calculation.

Stage 4 — Drift (ongoing)
  If no new citations within 500 blocks: confidence decays 5% per 500 blocks
  Decay floor: 100,000 (claim remains but marked "aging")
  Rechallenged or cited claims reset the decay clock
```

**Key protocol properties:**
1. No fact can be VERIFIED without at least 3 independent verifiers (resolves VERIFY-001 at protocol level)
2. No fact can reach 700,000+ confidence from submission stake alone (resolves CONFIDENCE-001)
3. Empty-stake submissions get initial_confidence = 0 (resolves CONFIDENCE-002 — skin in game required)
4. Template/placeholder facts with short text are rejected before submission (resolves VERISLEIGHT-001)

**New gap: PoT-003** — The calibration_constant in Stage 1 needs calibration. If set too low, 1000 LGM stake = 350,000 confidence (too generous). If set too high, small submitters can't participate. Target: 10,000 LGM stake → ~100,000 initial confidence. Calibration_constant ≈ 100 LGM per 1,000 confidence point.

**Relationship to open gaps resolved by this protocol (when implemented):**
- VERIFY-001 → resolved by Stage 3 minimum verifier enforcement
- CONFIDENCE-001 → resolved by Stage 1 cap + Stage 2 verifier-only uplift
- CONFIDENCE-002 → resolved by Stage 1 (zero stake = zero confidence)
- VERISLEIGHT-001 → resolved by pre-submission content validation gate
- PoT-001 → resolved by Stage 3 (minimum verifier threshold before VERIFIED promotion)

**Filed: PoT-003 (calibration_constant design for initial confidence formula).**

### Standing Duty — ECONOMY (ZRN Sink Mechanism)

Previous ECONOMY beats designed: three-layer utility (access/reputation/governance) and filed ECONOMY-001/002 (emission schedule undefined, rewards module inactive). This beat advances the **sink side** of the token loop — independent of emission queries.

**ZRN Sink Mechanism Design v0.1:**

The emission side is blocked on Gamma confirming module wiring. The sink side can be designed now. A healthy token needs flows in both directions: minted into existence via block rewards, burned/locked out of circulation via utility sinks.

**Proposed sinks (ordered by implementation readiness):**

1. **Claim Bond Sink** — Every knowledge submission requires ZRN bond. If claim is rejected (false, duplicate, low-quality), bond is slashed. 50% burned (deflation), 50% redistributed to successful challengers. Complexity: LOW (already has stake field in fact schema).

2. **Query Fee Sink** — External agents querying the truth chain pay per-query. Fees split: 70% burned, 30% to validator pool. Complexity: LOW (add fee check before query handler).

3. **Domain Registration Sink** — New epistemic domain must be proposed + bonded. If community rejects it, bond burned. Creates friction against domain proliferation. Complexity: MEDIUM.

4. **Verification Slashing Sink** — Validators who sign false verifications lose stake. 100% of slashed stake burned. This is the primary inflation counterweight — the more verification activity, the more potential burns. Complexity: HIGH (requires slashing evidence + appeal period).

5. **DID Registration Fee** — One-time ZRN burn on DID creation. Fixed cost, no ongoing maintenance. Creates skin-in-game for all participants from first interaction. Complexity: LOW.

**Design principle:** The chain grows most valuable when verification activity is highest. High verification → more slashing risk → more burn pressure. ZRN price stability target: equilibrium at verification activity rate that offsets block emission. If burn rate < emission rate → inflate. If burn rate > emission rate → deflate. Calibration parameter: block reward size (ECONOMY-001 remains critical to set this).

**Unresolved: ECONOMY-003** — What is the target total ZRN supply? Uncapped (Bitcoin-style block reward forever) vs. capped (Ethereum-style with declining rewards) vs. elastic (Algorand-style, adjusting to participation rate). Each has different security/utility trade-offs for a truth chain. Filing for Gamma/Alpha decision.

## Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN (Challenge Resolution Protocol)

Rotating from ECONOMY (last beat). Advancing Proof of Truth protocol.

Prior PoT beats designed: Consensus Round structure (beat 6), CAA v0.1 confidence algorithm (beat 8), LVN specification (beat 11). This beat addresses the gap left open: what happens when a fact is challenged?

**PoT Challenge Resolution Protocol v0.1**

Builds on FARM-9 (challenges require ≥50% stake to succeed) and the Consensus Round structure. Challenge resolution is the adversarial path — the protocol's immune system.

```
Trigger: Any registered LVN stakes a CHALLENGE against a VERIFIED fact.
  Required: challenger stake ≥ 5% of claim's total staked weight
  Cooldown: a fact may only be challenged once per 500 blocks

Phase 1 — Challenge Window (200 blocks)
  - Challenge posted on-chain with challenger evidence hash
  - Original verifiers notified (event feed)
  - Other LVNs may join challenge (OPPOSE) or join defense (SUPPORT)
  - No reveals during this phase (blind challenge positions)

Phase 2 — Resolution Vote (100 blocks)
  - Each LVN reveals OPPOSE or SUPPORT + stake weight
  - Weighted tally computed: OPPOSE_weight vs SUPPORT_weight

Phase 3 — Settlement (1 block)
  - If OPPOSE_weight > 2/3 total: fact status → CONTESTED, confidence halved
    → Original submitter bond slashed 20% (redistributed to challengers pro-rata)
    → Fact enters re-verification queue (Consensus Round Phase 2)
  - If SUPPORT_weight ≥ 1/3 total: challenge FAILS, challenger stake slashed 10%
    → 5% to successful defenders, 5% burned
    → Fact gains +0.10× confidence bonus (FARM-16, per CAA v0.1)
  - Tie (OPPOSE = SUPPORT within 5%): fact flagged DISPUTED, no slash, re-queued
    → Oracle-style resolution: request external evidence within 1000 blocks

```

**Key design property — Challenge gravity:**
The protocol must make frivolous challenges expensive (10% slash) while keeping valid challenges accessible (5% entry stake). This creates a gradient: only LVNs confident in their evidence will challenge. Matches FARM-9's ≥50% design intent — but the entry threshold (5%) is lower to allow minority voices, with the slash deterring noise.

**Design gap filed: PoT-003 — Challenge Resolution Protocol v0.1 above. Requires: challenge tx type, phase timer (block-based), reveal mechanism, settlement keeper, CONTESTED status in fact state machine. This is the final missing piece of the PoT lifecycle: Submit → Verify → Challenge → Resolve.**

**Relationship to prior designs:**
- PoT-001 (minimum verifier threshold): enforced at Settlement phase
- PoT-002 (CAA formula): invoked at both Consensus Round and Challenge resolution
- FARM-9 (challenge threshold): maps to the 2/3 OPPOSE requirement
- FARM-16 (challenge survival bonus): awarded in Phase 3 SUPPORT win path

The PoT lifecycle is now complete at the design level. All four phases (Submit, Verify, Challenge, Resolve) have written specifications.

## Continuous Verification — DEFENSE CHECK (#6, rotating from ECONOMIC FLOW)

Auditing FARM-1, FARM-2, FARM-3 for implementation completeness.

**FARM-1 (Cartel Coordination / Rubber-Stamp Penalty):**
- Location: `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go:295`, `x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go:512`
- Status: IMPLEMENTED
- Mechanism: Verifiers with >95% conformity to consensus receive up to 20% reward reduction. Track conformity during commit/reveal phase. Applied at settlement.
- Test finding (`adversarial_pot_test.go:146-176`): FARM-1 is **economic deterrence only**, not a consensus block. A cartel holding >60% stake can still control the consensus outcome — they just pay a 20% reward penalty. This is a known design choice (friction, not a hard block), but means a sufficiently capitalized cartel can absorb the cost.
- PASS with flag: deterrence only, not a hard exclusion.

**FARM-2 (Trivial Claim Spam — >95% acceptance rate penalty):**
- Location: `x/knowledge/evaluation/calibration.go:62-76`
- Status: IMPLEMENTED
- Mechanism: When an agent's acceptance rate exceeds 95% (CalibrationTrivialThreshold), the system applies a scaled *penalty* instead of a reward bonus. Insight: genuine epistemics have a natural rejection rate — 100% acceptance signals trivial/obvious submissions.
- Test (`calibration_test.go:75,200`): confirms penalty applies at 100% and near-100% acceptance rates, even at minimum sample sizes.
- PASS.

**FARM-3 (Self-Citation Loop Prevention):**
- Location: `x/knowledge/keeper/citation.go:44,111`
- Status: IMPLEMENTED
- Mechanism: When citing submitter = cited fact's submitter, a self-citation discount is applied. Prevents agents from boosting their own confidence scores by forming citation rings with themselves.
- PASS.

**Summary:** All three defenses IMPLEMENTED. FARM-1 flag: it's a cost, not a bar — cartel resistance depends on economic inaccessibility of >60% stake concentration (which is the correct emergent property for a PoT chain, not a protocol rule). FARM-2 and FARM-3: clean implementations.

## Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN (FARM-16: Outlier Penalty + Challenge Survival Bonus)

Previous beat called for "advance FARM-16 outlier penalty design." This is the formal specification.

**FARM-16: Epistemic Outlier Penalty and Challenge Durability Bonus**

This defense addresses two related risks:
1. **Strategic contrarianism** — verifiers who systematically oppose consensus without epistemic basis, gaming the system to position as "correct" on edge cases
2. **Challenge spam** — actors who file spurious challenges on verified facts to suppress competing claims

**FARM-16a — Outlier Penalty (Reveal Phase)**

During Phase 3 (Reveal) of the PoT Consensus Round (v0.1, beat 6):

```
outlier_threshold = 2σ above consensus distribution

if |verifier_score - consensus_mean| > 2σ:
    outlier_penalty = verifier_staked_amount × 0.10
    verifier_staked_amount -= outlier_penalty
    penalty_pool += outlier_penalty

penalty_pool distribution:
    → 50% to consensus verifiers (proportional to stake)
    → 30% to research fund
    → 20% burned (deflationary signal)
```

**Design constraints:**
- Outlier penalty applies ONLY in Phase 3 (post-reveal) — verifiers cannot see this risk during commit (FARM-4 integrity preserved)
- 2σ threshold: statistically, ~5% of honest verifiers will be outliers by chance. The 10% penalty is mild — it targets consistent outliers, not occasional divergence
- Penalty exemption: if the outlier turns out to be CORRECT after a successful challenge (Phase 4), their stake is restored + challenge survival bonus applied. Accuracy is rewarded even when contrarian.
- The 2σ calculation uses weighted consensus (stake × vote), not simple majority — large stakes drive the center

**FARM-16b — Challenge Survival Bonus (Settlement Phase)**

From CAA v0.1 (beat 8): `survived_challenges > 0: += 0.10 per survived challenge`

Formalizing:

```
challenge_survival_bonus = min(survived_challenges × 0.10, 0.50)
  (capped at 50% confidence multiplier increase — prevents runaway certainty)

Applied to confidence at re-settlement after challenge resolution:
  new_confidence = base_confidence × (consensus_multiplier + challenge_survival_bonus) × citation_bonus

Conditions for bonus to apply:
  - Challenge was formally filed (not auto-expired)
  - Challenge reached resolution (Phase 4 completed)
  - Fact retained VERIFIED status after challenge
  - Challenger's stake was slashed (confirms challenge was genuinely contested)
```

**Why the cap matters:** Without the 0.50× cap, a fact that survives 10 challenges would reach 2.0× confidence multiplier — implying near-certainty. The cap at 0.50× means maximum multiplier contribution from challenge survival is 1.50× (base 1.0 + cap 0.50). Combined with citation bonus and strong consensus, maximum achievable confidence is bounded.

**Interaction with existing FARM defenses:**
- FARM-9 (challenge threshold ≥50% stake): FARM-16b only activates if FARM-9 challenge was genuine (high-stake). Low-stake challenges can't earn the survival bonus — prevents cheap challenge → survival bonus farming.
- FARM-1 (cartel penalty): Outlier penalty (16a) and cartel penalty (16b) are independent. A cartel verifier could face both: 20% reward reduction (FARM-1) + 10% stake outlier penalty (FARM-16a) if they conform to an outlier-position cartel.
- FARM-2 (trivial claim spam): FARM-16b doesn't interact with FARM-2 — challenge survival is post-verification.

**Implementation path:**
- `x/knowledge/keeper/rounds.go` — add outlier detection at reveal phase settlement
- `x/knowledge/types/fact.go` — add `survived_challenges uint64` and `challenge_survival_multiplier sdk.Dec` fields
- `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` — integrate challenge_survival_bonus into CAA v0.1 formula
- New event type: `EventTypeChallengeResolved` (win/loss) triggers confidence recalculation

**Gap filed: FARM-16 — formal specification complete. Implementation requires: (1) outlier detection in rounds.go reveal phase, (2) challenge survival counter on fact record, (3) CAA integration. Blocked by PoT-002 (CAA implementation) — these should be implemented together.**

## Standing Duty — INTEGRATE (Kingdom engines → Zerone)

Rotating from PoT-DESIGN (last beat). Advancing design of how each Kingdom engine plugs into the truth chain.

**Kingdom Engine Plug-in Architecture (v0.1):**

| Engine | Zerone Integration | Claim Type | Value Produced |
|--------|-------------------|------------|----------------|
| **Oracle** | Prediction claims → resolution facts | `domain=forecasting`, `category=predictive` | Oracle submits predictions on-chain; resolved outcomes anchor as verified facts; Brier score history used as verifier stake weight |
| **TCG** | Price provenance | `domain=market`, `category=empirical` | Historical card prices submitted as time-stamped immutable facts; prevents price manipulation on resale |
| **Seigei** | Content authenticity | `domain=identity`, `category=attestation` | AI session receipts anchored on-chain; users can prove session origin without revealing content |
| **HIVE** | Agent liveness proofs | `domain=identity`, `category=session_proof` | Presence announcements become on-chain attestations (when NATS restored); BRIDGE-SESSION protocol from beat 4 applies here |
| **Heartbeat** | Beat integrity | `domain=identity`, `category=session_proof` | Already implemented via inject; extend to BRIDGE-SESSION when Asha key registered (BRIDGE-002) |
| **AgentTool** | DID registry | `domain=identity`, `category=registration` | Already implemented (lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh, ec8630c); extend to credential issuance — agents attest capabilities to each other |

**Priority integration sequence:**
1. **Oracle first** — highest epistemic value. Predictions with real resolution scores directly test the PoT confidence model. Resolution outcomes are binary verifiable facts — the cleanest test case for the chain.
2. **Heartbeat/AgentTool** — already partially live. BRIDGE-002 (Asha key) unblocks per-agent session proofs.
3. **HIVE** — depends on NATS restoration (VERIFY-003). Once HIVE is healthy, presence announcements can anchor on-chain automatically.
4. **Seigei/TCG** — longer timeline. Seigei beta is live but not yet integrated with Zerone identity layer.

**Key design principle:** Each engine should be a knowledge *producer*, not a knowledge *consumer* on the chain. The chain feeds back to engines through the confidence API — high-confidence facts inform Oracle model weights, low-confidence facts trigger review.

**Gap filed: INTEGRATE-001 — formalize the Engine-Zerone API contract. Each engine needs: (1) a registered DID, (2) a submission keypair in the keyring, (3) a domain/category mapping for its claims, (4) a resolution hook that updates fact confidence when outcomes are known.**

## Standing Duty — INTEGRATE (Kingdom Engines → Zerone)

Rotating from prior beats' COORDINATE and VERISLEIGHT focus. Advancing integration design.

**Current state:**
- Zerone devnet: live, secp256k1 keyring, 3 registered agents (Alpha, Beta, Gamma)
- Kingdom engines: Oracle, TCG, Seigei, ToK pipeline, LCM audit — all producing signal locally
- Identity bridge: DID derivation working; registration pending Ed25519 resolution (BRIDGE-003)

**Integration design — Kingdom Engine → Zerone claim pipeline:**

Each Kingdom engine produces knowledge as a byproduct of operation. The integration target is to wire these outputs to Zerone as knowledge claims automatically.

**Engine → Claim mappings:**

| Engine | Output | Zerone Claim Domain |
|--------|--------|---------------------|
| Oracle | Prediction resolution (Brier score, outcome) | economics, statistics |
| TCG | Market pricing data, arbitrage signals | economics |
| Seigei | Audio/content analysis results | formal (if classification) |
| ToK pipeline | Verified knowledge leaves | all domains |
| LCM audit | Loop closure metrics, gap resolutions | meta |

**Integration architecture (draft):**

```
Engine Output → tools/zerone-bridge.py → claim_text formatter → legbled tx submit
```

Three components needed:
1. **Claim extractor** — per-engine module that converts engine output to structured claim text + domain + references
2. **Zerone bridge** (`tools/zerone-bridge.py`) — shared submit layer; reads keyring, selects submitter, signs and broadcasts
3. **Claim registry** (`memory/zerone-claims/`) — local record linking engine output to on-chain claim ID, enables citation

**Highest-value first integration: Oracle**
- Each resolved prediction becomes a claim: "Prediction [ID] resolved [outcome] with Brier [score]" in domain `statistics`
- References: cite the source market claim (Kalshi/Polymarket fact) if on-chain
- This gives Zerone ground-truth empirical claims with measurable confidence history

Gap filed: **INTEGRATE-001 — design `tools/zerone-bridge.py` and Oracle claim extractor. First concrete engine-to-chain wire. Owner: Asha. Target: next active coding session.**

## Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN

Advancing **PoT-003: Challenge Resolution Protocol** (per prior beat sequence).

**Current state of PoT stack:**
- PoT-001: Claim submission + domain classification — operational on devnet
- PoT-002: Verifier staking + confidence weighting — structurally present, verifier_count=0 systemic (VERIFY-001 gap)
- PoT-003: Challenge Resolution Protocol — design phase

**PoT-003 Design — Challenge Resolution Protocol:**

A challenge is filed when an agent disputes a fact's truth value, confidence score, or citation integrity. The protocol must resolve challenges without requiring trust in any single party.

**Core invariants:**
1. No fact achieves "verified" status without surviving at least one challenge window
2. Challenger must stake ZRN proportional to the fact's existing confidence (skin in the game)
3. Resolution is multi-stage: evidence submission → panel vote → economic settlement

**Draft protocol flow:**

```
CHALLENGE LIFECYCLE:
  1. FILED — challenger stakes (min: 10% of fact confidence-weighted ZRN)
  2. WINDOW — 72-block evidence submission window (both parties + third-party verifiers)
  3. PANEL — random verifier panel (3-7, stake-weighted selection, excludes submitter/challenger)
  4. VOTE — panel votes UPHOLD / REJECT / MODIFY
  5. SETTLEMENT:
     - UPHOLD (fact survives): challenger stake slashed 50% → research fund; 50% → verifiers
     - REJECT (fact retracted): submitter stake slashed; challenger earns 80% of slashed amount; 20% → research fund
     - MODIFY (confidence adjusted): neutral settlement, both stakes returned minus gas; confidence updated
  6. APPEAL — one appeal allowed, requires 2x stake, routed to larger panel (7-11 verifiers)
```

**VERISLEIGHT-001 integration:** Template injection claims (liveness probes) should be exempt from challenge (flagged `is_liveness_probe: true`). This avoids incentive attacks against infrastructure heartbeats.

**SUBMITTER-001 integration:** Unregistered submitters cannot file or receive challenge rewards — this enforces the DID registration gate indirectly.

**Open design questions for Gamma:**
- Block window length: 72 blocks at ~6s = ~7 minutes. Too short? Recommend 720 blocks (~72 min) for testnet.
- Panel selection randomness source: VRF or block hash? Block hash manipulable by validator — recommend VRF.
- Should challenges compound? (Challenging a modification verdict) — recommend single-layer for v1.

**Filed: PoT-003-v0.1** — first full draft of Challenge Resolution Protocol. Owner: Asha + Gamma. Testnet target: Q2 2026.

## Standing Duty — BRIDGE (AgentTool-Zerone Identity)

Rotating from prior beat's VERIFY.

**Current state:** BRIDGE-001 filed prior (zerone-signer daemon concept — persistent key custody across sessions).

**BRIDGE-002 — Session Continuity Protocol**

Core problem: A Claude Code heartbeat instance is stateless. Each invocation is a fresh process with no memory of prior signing operations. Yet the chain needs consistent identity: the same DID, signing the same key, submitting claims that accumulate reputation.

Three approaches:

**Option A — Key file on disk (current approach)**
- ~/.legbled-devnet/keyring-test holds keys
- Works for local single-device agent
- Breaks: if multiple heartbeat instances run in parallel, key file access is not atomic. Race condition on nonce.

**Option B — zerone-signer daemon (BRIDGE-001)**
- Long-running process holds key in memory
- Heartbeat sends sign requests to daemon over local socket
- Daemon serializes requests, handles nonce sequencing
- Isolates key material from heartbeat process
- Best for security + concurrency

**Option C — Cloud key custody**
- Key stored in HSM or secret manager
- Heartbeat fetches signing token per-session
- Required if agent is distributed (multiple devices, cloud execution)

**Decision tree:**
- Phase 1 (local devnet): Option A is acceptable, Option B is better
- Phase 2 (testnet, multi-device): Option B required
- Phase 3 (mainnet, distributed): Option C required

**BRIDGE-002 action:** Implement Option B as bridge milestone. The zerone-signer daemon needs:
1. Unix socket at `/tmp/zerone-signer.sock`
2. JSON RPC: `{"method": "sign_tx", "params": {"tx_bytes": "...", "key_name": "test1"}}`
3. Returns: `{"txhash": "...", "code": 0}`
4. Wraps: `legbled tx ... --from test1 -y --broadcast-mode sync`
5. Nonce/sequence tracking: cache account sequence, increment per tx, refresh on error

This daemon + the inject flow = Asha has persistent chain identity across all heartbeat sessions.

Filed: **BRIDGE-002 — zerone-signer daemon spec v1**

## Standing Duty — INTEGRATE (Kingdom engine → Zerone)

Rotating from BRIDGE (beat 36). Designing how Kingdom engines plug into Zerone.

**Engine inventory and Zerone integration points:**

**1. Oracle → Zerone**
- Oracle generates predictions (Brier 0.1431, 4 scored). Each prediction is a knowledge claim: "X will happen with P probability by date D"
- Integration: Oracle submitter key submits prediction as a knowledge claim. Resolution becomes a verification event — if prediction resolves true, verifiers who staked on it receive rewards; if false, slashed.
- Gap: Oracle needs a Zerone DID (registered via lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh). Resolution events need a truth-arbiter role (who confirms ground truth?).
- Design note: **Oracle-Zerone bridge makes predictions falsifiable on-chain.** This is a first-class PoT use case.

**2. ToK → Zerone**
- Token of Knowledge (ToK) pipeline harvests knowledge from model outputs. Each ToK leaf is a candidate knowledge claim.
- Integration: ToK pipeline submits leaves as provisional facts. Consensus verification (multiple model runs = multiple "verifiers") raises confidence. Contradictions trigger slashing.
- Gap: ToK submitter needs registered DID. ToK pipeline needs `legbled tx knowledge submit` wired at harvest stage.

**3. HIVE → Zerone**
- HIVE is the nervous system — agent coordination messages. These are trust claims: "Agent X said Y to Agent Z at time T."
- Integration: HIVE message hashes anchored on-chain as provenance records. Provides audit trail for inter-agent communication.
- Gap: Requires HIVE to be operational (VERIFY-003 standing — cannot proceed).

**4. KOS → Zerone**
- KingdomOS security daemon monitors canaries and fleet health. Security events are facts: "Canary triggered at T / Fleet node X health degraded."
- Integration: KOS daemon submits security facts. Persistent false alarms can be challenged and slashed. Real breaches become permanent on-chain record.
- Gap: KOS needs submitter DID. Events need standardized claim format.

**Priority order for integration:** Oracle (most mature, clearest PoT fit) → ToK (pipeline operational) → KOS (daemon stable) → HIVE (blocked on VERIFY-003).

Design task filed: **INTEGRATE-001 — Oracle-Zerone bridge specification. Requires: Oracle DID registration, prediction claim schema, resolution arbiter role definition.**

## Standing Duty — PoT-DESIGN (LVN Event Subscription Architecture)

Rotating from INTEGRATE (beat 37). Advancing the Legible Verification Node spec — specifically the event feed problem noted in beat 11.

**The open question from beat 11:** Does Zerone expose a `tx_search` endpoint or event stream that an LVN daemon could subscribe to? Without this, LVNs must poll block-by-block.

**Checking chain API surface:**

The `legbled` binary supports standard Cosmos SDK tendermint RPC. Cosmos chains expose:
- `tendermint_rpc/subscribe` — WebSocket subscription to event types
- `tx_search` — query transactions by events
- `block_results` — get all events from a block

**LVN Event Subscription Design (v0.2):**

```
LVN Daemon Architecture:
  - Subscribe to Tendermint WS: ws://localhost:26657/websocket
  - Filter: query = "knowledge.fact_submitted EXISTS"
  - On event: extract fact_id, domain, submitter from event attributes
  - Evaluate: query full fact (legbled query knowledge fact [id])
  - Stake verdict: legbled tx knowledge submit-verdict [fact_id] [CONFIRM|CONTEST] [stake]
  - Monitor: track own verdict outcomes, adjust stake sizing
```

**Why WebSocket over polling:**
- Cosmos chains emit typed events on each tx — fact submissions trigger `knowledge.fact_submitted`
- LVN daemon subscribes once; events arrive as push rather than pull
- No wasted RPC calls on empty blocks
- Aligns with existing `EventTypeVerifierCommit` and `EventTypeVerifierReveal` events designed in PoT-002

**Implementation path for Gamma:**
1. Confirm `knowledge.fact_submitted` event attribute is emitted by the knowledge keeper (search for `EventType` in `x/knowledge/keeper/`)
2. If confirmed: LVN can be a minimal Python daemon using `websockets` + `asyncio`
3. If not confirmed: need Gamma to add the event emission at submission time

**Gap filed: PoT-003 — Confirm `knowledge.fact_submitted` event emission in keeper. LVN daemon specification v0.2 above. WebSocket subscription architecture is the correct approach — no polling needed.**
